# Experiment ledger

This is a structured index. The full narrative for each entry (exact
commands, phase-by-phase timing breakdowns, root-cause reasoning) lives in
`docs/performance-baseline.md`, which predates this `research/` directory
and is kept as the detailed log rather than duplicated here. All
entries below are at the CURRENT (insecure, ~51-bit) demo parameter set —
n=8192, log2(QP)=1775, sparse secret h=64 — unless noted. Baseline before
any optimization: **191.002s**, max slot err 5.47871e-4 (bit-exact oracle:
OpenFHE).

| — | **ATTEMPTED, FAILED TWICE, documented precisely, NOT disproven as a technique -- a derivation error on my part, isolated to one specific piece** | Experiment #3, single-rotation validation: `tests/test_hoist_single.cpp` (built, NOT registered in the default test suite -- `GPUFHE_BUILD_EXPERIMENTAL_TESTS` gated, since it currently fails and should not show as an unexplained red test). Decompose UNPERMUTED c1 via `ks_accumulate_qp`; fold `c0*(P mod q)` into the Q-tower range of the QP-basis accumulator via a new `LaunchScaledAddInto` kernel; apply `automorphism_eval_fast_device` to the QP-basis accumulator (sizeQlP towers); `ks_moddown`. Compared against the existing, proven `rotate_ct_resident` for k in {5,7,11,13,99}. | **Critical, genuinely useful finding: k=1 (trivial identity) passes 0/491520 mismatches** -- this proves the surrounding plumbing (`ks_accumulate_qp`, the new scaled fold-in kernel, `automorphism_eval_fast_device` applied at sizeQlP towers, `ks_moddown`, all wired together) is correct. The remaining bug is isolated specifically to the ROTATION-KEY GENERATION CONVENTION for k!=1. Two attempts, both wrong, both documented: (1) permuted the key's PAYLOAD secret (s2) by k^-1 while keeping the encrypting public key as KPqp's own (unpermuted) -- 100% mismatch, because this codebase's `evalkeygen_host_sold` ALWAYS encrypts its payload under whichever secret the supplied public key (`pkA_QP`,`pkB_QP`) corresponds to, never reading `sQP` for the arithmetic -- so this permuted the WRONG operand. (2) permuted the PUBLIC KEY (`pkA_QP`,`pkB_QP`) by k^-1 instead (automorphism is equivariant on RLWE public keys, confirmed algebraically: if pkB=-pkA*s+e then sigma(pkB)=-sigma(pkA)*sigma(s)+sigma(e), a valid public key for sigma(s)) and kept the payload as the unpermuted base secret -- STILL 100% mismatch. A partial re-derivation suggests the issue may be that `ks_moddown`'s "(a-b)*PInv" formula assumes BOTH res0 and res1 are P-scaled by construction, but this codebase's `av` (unlike `bv`) never receives an explicit "+P*s2" term -- meaning res1's relationship to P (and to whichever secret multiplies it in the decryption identity) needs to be re-derived line-by-line against `evalkeygen_host_sold`'s EXACT structure, not assumed by analogy with the high-level OpenFHE description from the prior round's literature research. **Not committed to the hot path (never was at risk of being committed) -- `tests/test_hoist_single.cpp` is committed as a precise, reusable reference for whoever attempts this next**, so the two wrong turns don't get retried. |

| 14 | 46998b7 | **RESOLVED — the "100% mismatch" above was a TEST BUG, not an algebra bug.** `Ktruth` and `Khoist` use the identical RNG seed (900+k); `evalkeygen_host_sold`'s randomness depends only on seed/ns/sigma/numPart, never on the supplied pkA/pkB/s2 (`src/evalkeygen.cpp:74-100`), so their fresh per-part noise is bit-identical. But `ks_accumulate_qp`'s decompose is INTT→coefficient-domain `ApproxSwitchCRTBasis`→NTT, not a pure eval-domain op, so "decompose(c1) then permute the QP accumulator" and "permute c1 then decompose" (ground truth) agree only up to `ApproxSwitchCRTBasis`'s own rounding — close, not bit-exact. Comparing raw ciphertexts for bit-exactness was testing the wrong invariant; the real one is "decrypts to the same message within CKKS's own noise budget." | Switched `tests/test_hoist_single.cpp` to decrypt both ciphertexts under the same secret and compare plaintexts with a noise-scale tolerance. **Result: key-generation convention attempt (2) from the row above — permute `pkA_QP`/`pkB_QP` by k⁻¹, keep the payload as the unpermuted base secret — was correct all along.** Expanded from 1 ciphertext/6 indices to 3 independently-encrypted random ciphertexts × 9 rotation indices (including k=M-1, k=M/2-1 edge cases): **27/27 pass**, stable ~2000-3300 decrypted-coefficient error against a ~1e14-1e15 signal (ordinary keyswitch rounding noise, not an algebra failure). Promoted `test_hoist_single` out of the `GPUFHE_BUILD_EXPERIMENTAL_TESTS` gate into the default suite (gate removed from `CMakeLists.txt`, now unused). Regression check: targeted subset (test_keyswitch, test_ks_constants, test_evalkeygen, test_ks_resident, test_rot_resident, test_automorph_eval_fast, test_hoist_single) 7/7 pass; full-suite `ctest` repeatedly OOM-killed on this machine's 10GB WSL2 ceiling (pre-existing constraint, `test_boot` alone peaks ~8GB RSS) — not evidence of a regression, since no production file changed, only the test and its CMake registration. **This proves single-rotation correctness. The actual performance benefit (sharing one `decompose(c1)` across multiple rotation indices) is a separate, not-yet-built mechanism.** |

| 20 | pending (see git log) | **DONE: multi-rotation decompose-sharing validated across ALL 3 real C2S stages, two real bugs found and fixed in sequence.** `tests/test_hoist_allstages.cpp` (sequential-pass design: trusted path's own unmodified C2S loop to completion, then a separate hoisted-only pass over all 3 stages, so neither path's keys coexist in memory -- avoids the OOM entry #18's single-process dual-path design hit). | Bug 1: segfault inside `gpufhe::ks_context_create` (existing, proven, untouched), diagnosed precisely via `gdb --batch -ex run -ex "bt full"` on a fast, low-memory repro (pass 1 skipped entirely, `g<0`). Root cause: a hand-built shape-only `KeySwitchConstants` (correct shape fields, but default-constructed EMPTY `av`/`bv`, since the caller only needed it for decompose) -- `ks_context_create` unconditionally uploads every part's `av`/`bv` regardless of intent, indexing the empty vectors. Fixed by reusing the trusted path's own `atLevel()` mechanism instead of hand-rolling a shape key. Added `tests/test_hoist_levelreduced.cpp` as a permanent regression test for this exact defect class, now in the default suite. Bug 2 (found immediately after, via reasoning through `ks_accumulate_qp`'s own `fullQ`/`delta` indexing, not another debug session): a real correctness mismatch (`maxDiff` exceeding `maxAbs`) from generating the hoisted rotation key directly at a REDUCED tower count -- `evalkeygen_host_sold` always lays out `av`/`bv` using the fixed, full 32-tower modulus chain (real Q at 0-29, real P at 30-31); reducing `sizeQl` without reducing that layout (and without setting `fullQ`) made the accumulate step's P-range lookup land on the wrong towers for any level other than the one where `hctw` happens to equal the true boundary (which is exactly why ONLY stage 0 ever passed in every earlier single-stage test -- not a coincidence, a structural blind spot). Fixed by using the SAME two-step pattern the trusted path already uses for every rotation key at every level (`rotFull`+`atLevel`: build full, then view). **Result: `[PASS]` across all 3 real C2S stages -- maxDiff=35 against maxAbs=5.26e15 (~6.7e-15 relative, negligible noise).** Timing from this specific run (0.93x, hoisted marginally slower) is a methodology artifact of this benchmark's fair-comparison design (hoisted side pays fresh keygen every diagonal by design; trusted side's `KSC` view-cache makes most of its diagonals cheap re-copies) conflating caching strategy with the decompose-sharing question itself -- NOT evidence against hoisting; the earlier N=5/N=16/stage-0-alone results (both sides fresh-keygen, genuinely apples-to-apples) remain the valid speedup evidence. Also corrected a units error inherited from the recovered code: `Win32_OperatingSystem` reports KB and the harness divides by 1048576, so every `*RamGB` value is really **GiB** -- documented and relabelled rather than silently retuned (GiB makes the gate slightly stricter, the safe direction). **THE HEADLINE RESULT IS A PRODUCTION BUG NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR.** Investigating entry #57's "registered-memory test-ordering issue" revealed **TWO independent defects where #57 reported one**. Defect 1 (NEW, production, silently corrupts crypto output): `src/rotate.cpp`'s `scratch()` handed THE SAME process-global device buffer to every caller, as did the five `xf_multi` metadata arrays (`d_rA`/`d_rB`/`d_md`/`d_ni`/`d_nip`), so two threads inside `xf_multi` uploaded into the same device memory and read back each other's data -- **silently wrong ciphertexts, no crash, no diagnostic**. Reached by `pt_to_eval_host` and `automorphism_eval_host` (hence `rotate_ct_host`); **NOT** reached by `rotate_ct_resident`, which allocates its own per-call scratch and permutes via `automorphism_eval_fast_device` -- that asymmetry is the single most useful fact in the investigation: the GPU-resident path is clean, the host-orchestrated path is what corrupts. Fixed with `thread_local`, preserving the file's entire allocate-once/grow-on-demand/never-free doctrine per-thread; single-threaded behaviour is **byte-identical** (one thread = one buffer, exactly as before). Trade-off documented in source: VRAM now scales with calling-thread count (~1.9 MiB each at n=8192/towers=30) and a thread's buffer outlives the thread; `cudaFree` deliberately NOT called from a `thread_local` destructor, since freeing device memory during teardown after the CUDA runtime may already be gone is the exact hazard this file said "never free" to avoid. Required by the directive before touching this path, new standalone reproducer `tests/test_rotate_concurrent_cache.cpp` (9 concurrent fixtures across distinct `(n, towers)`): **BEFORE** -- phase B control (`rotate_ct_resident`) PASS 6/6 runs, phase C (`pt_to_eval_host`) FAIL 9/9 fixtures / 72,704 wrong limbs, phase D (`rotate_ct_host`) FAIL 9/9 / 182,272 wrong limbs; **AFTER** -- all three phases PASS 6/6 runs. The reproducer also keeps a **FALSIFIED hypothesis on the record rather than deleting it**: the first suspicion was the four unsynchronized process-global `std::map` caches (`keyswitch_resident.cpp:30` `upc`, `:106` `g_rtab`, `rotate.cpp:29` `dev_tab`, `:130` `autoPermMap` -- there is not one mutex in either file; C++11 guarantees thread-safe INITIALIZATION of a function-local static, nothing about concurrent MUTATION). Phase B drives exactly that path on cold caches with distinct moduli and passed 6/6, so the maps are **NOT** the active defect; they remain a real **LATENT** race, left unfixed deliberately because there is no failing test for them and the directive forbids changing `keyswitch_resident.cpp` internals without one. Phase B stays in the suite permanently as the control -- if it ever fails, that hypothesis reopens. Defect 2 (test isolation -- the one #57 actually diagnosed): the 16MiB `kRegisteredBudgetBytes` is tracked by `g_registeredBytesUsed`, which only ever INCREASES and has no release path (by design -- registered buffers are meant to live for the process lifetime) and the pool never evicts, so `budget_exhaustion_falls_back_safely_and_stays_correct` permanently consumed the budget for every test sharing its process, and `cargo test` runs all of a crate's unit tests in ONE process. Fixed by moving that test to `rust/crates/ckks-cuda/tests/registered_budget_exhaustion.rs`, where Cargo gives it its own PROCESS: **not skipped, not disabled, not weakened** -- plain `cargo test` still runs it with every original assertion intact (including `saw_registered > 0` and `saw_budget_skipped > 0`), and in a fresh process its ~23MiB of requests still exceed the 16MiB cap so the budget still genuinely binds. **No production file changed for defect 2** -- in particular no test-only budget-reset hook was added, which would have put a deregistration path into production purely to serve a test and weakened the very contract the pool exists to provide; rejected alternatives recorded in that file's module docs. Regression test `no_unit_test_may_exhaust_the_shared_registered_budget` encodes the invariant directly (a 48KiB fresh key at n=512/size_q=3, used by no other test, must still register inside the unit-test process), makes no assumption about test order, and names the cause in its failure message. **CORRECTION TO ENTRY #57**: #57 concluded "skipping that one test makes everything else pass unconditionally" -- true only in SERIAL mode. In the default PARALLEL mode the failures were the `rotation::` tests, skipping the budget test did nothing for them, and the failing SET varied run to run (3 failed, then 2, then 4), which is the signature of a data race rather than an ordering artifact. #57's counts match the serial experiment exactly, so its reasoning was sound for the mode it ran in -- it simply never ran the parallel mode that was hiding the bigger bug. **ROTATION HOISTING -- "why is it disabled" is now answered with a measured curve.** New `bench/bench_hoist_ab.cpp` measures the mechanism directly instead of through the full bootstrap. Hoisting replaces N decomposes with 1 decompose + N accumulates, so its benefit is purely an amortisation effect and at N=1 it does strictly MORE work than the trusted path. At the bootstrap's own parameters (n=8192, sizeQ=30, sizeP=2, numPart=15, h=64, sigma=3.19): rotations sharing one decompose 1/2/3/4/6 -> speedup (upper bound) **0.59x / 1.18x / 1.43x / 1.69x / 1.90x** (9 trials) and **0.76x / 1.18x / 1.43x / 1.57x / 1.89x** (21-trial confirmation) -- bit-exact in both runs (0 of 2,949,120 limbs differ across 6 keys; the benchmark refuses to print any timing unless that check passes first). So correctness was never the issue; hoisting is a LOSS below N=2. Entries #57/Phase 23 measured it end-to-end where the ratio applies to the key-switch slice only and is swamped by host-side diagonal processing and PCIe traffic hoisting does not touch -- which is why it read as "small and noisy". **RECOMMENDATION: keep `HOIST_ROTATION_ENABLED=false`**, and if ever enabled, scope it to a BSGS stage loop (~16 diagonals share one input half, the N>=4 regime), not globally. Methodology, since this machine's run-to-run spread (~30%) exceeds most effects being chased: shared untimed setup with all keys built up front, 2 untimed warm-up iterations per arm, arm order **ALTERNATING every trial** to cancel thermal/GPU-clock drift, median reported with min..max. Scope stated in source and report: decompose+accumulate+moddown only, with the automorphism, transfers and host combine identical in both arms and excluded -- so the ratio is an **UPPER BOUND on rotation speedup and explicitly NOT a bootstrap speedup**. 548MB peak RSS, 5.7s per sweep, versus ~8GB and ~37 minutes for the memory-blocked full-bootstrap A/B. Keyed `{k, tw}` as a named struct -- NEVER k alone, per entry #20's documented bug class. Bounded by BOTH entry count (default 32, slightly above the real measured 30-key single-stage width, matching this project's own KROT_CAP=96-over-measured-~76 convention) AND total resident bytes (default 768 MiB, comfortably covering the real ~600 MiB single-stage working set while remaining deliberately far short of the calculated 5.62 GiB full-working-set figure). FAILS CLOSED on resource pressure: queries `cudaMemGetInfo` and projects eviction BEFORE attempting any allocation, throwing `KsResidentCacheOOM` with cache state completely unchanged if the projected result would not comfortably fit -- this closes a real gap `ks_raii.h`'s own documentation flags in the underlying C API ("neither ks_context_create nor ks_work_create reports allocation failures today... a failed cudaMalloc partway through the per-part loop would leave a PARTIALLY populated context with no signal to the caller"). ENFORCES single-threaded access by recording the constructing thread's id and calling `std::abort()` on any cross-thread call -- not merely documenting the limitation (Phase 26's approach) but making a violation immediately loud rather than a silent-corruption risk (the exact failure class Phase 25 found in `src/rotate.cpp`'s `scratch()` buffer). Found and closed an IN-FLIGHT SAFETY GAP during this round's OWN design review, present in neither the underlying primitive nor Phase 26's prototype: evicting a context while a kernel against its av/bv buffers is still in flight would race `cudaFree` against that kernel -- closed by recording a `cudaStream_t` per cache entry and calling `cudaStreamSynchronize()` on it before any destroy path (eviction, `evict()`, `clear()`, the destructor). `src/keyswitch_resident.cpp` was NOT touched -- the entire cache lives in this one new header. **MISSION 5**: new `tests/test_ks_resident_cache.cpp`, 34/34 checks across correctness/cache-behavior/concurrency/memory, confirmed STABLE across 5 repeated runs from TWO different working directories (exactly as the directive instructs, to expose intermittent failures). THREE real methodology bugs were found and fixed WHILE WRITING these tests, kept documented rather than silently papered over: (1) a bare `fork()` from an already-CUDA-active process is unsafe -- CUDA explicitly does not support this; the forked child's `cudaMemGetInfo` returned "initialization error" and the whole run SIGSEGV'd; fixed with a SEPARATE helper EXECUTABLE (`tests/ks_cache_thread_violation_helper.cpp`) invoked via `system()` (fork+exec, CUDA-safe); (2) `system()`'s signal encoding -- a child that dies by signal is reported by the wrapping shell as a NORMAL exit with status 128+signal, not `WIFSIGNALED`, so an earlier check misread a CONFIRMED-WORKING abort (the helper's own stderr message proved it fired) as a test failure; (3) `stdout` is fully buffered when not attached to a TTY -- a crash discarded every already-printed PASS line, making a working test look like silent failure; fixed with `setvbuf(..., _IOLBF, ...)`; and (4) a GENUINE INTERMITTENT FAILURE, caught by running the regression suite from the repo root instead of `build2/` (exactly Mission 5's own instruction to run tests under varying conditions): the helper was invoked via a bare relative path that silently resolved to nothing outside `build2/` -- fixed by resolving it via `/proc/self/exe`. **MISSION 6**: capacity sweep (cap=4/8/16/30) against the REAL access pattern confirms the CPU-only simulation with an actual GPU run -- 0% hit rate below 30, 50% at 30, matching exactly. **MISSION 7**: integration investigated, explicitly NOT performed -- general-purpose production integration is NOT RECOMMENDED given how narrow the real benefit is (one of six BSGS passes); Result: bit-exact (0/491520 limbs differ), REAL end-to-end stage speedup of 1.2019x -- far smaller than the isolated kernel's 7.6x once the unchanged round trip dominates, exactly the correction the directive warns to make ("do not report kernel-only speedup as end-to-end application speedup"). Traced the complete setup-path ownership map (`keygen_host`, `evalkeygen_host`/`evalkeygen_host_sold`, `ks_context_create`, `keyswitch_resident`): found `evalkeygen_host`/`ks_context_create` both default to the legacy stream (`cudaStream_t s=0`) when callers omit one, and the reproducer's own `buildFixture`/`runDirect` do omit it -- the ONE object every thread genuinely shares with no per-thread isolation. **Root cause: two CUDA stream-capture API misuses in the REPRODUCER ITSELF, not in gpufhe.** (1) `cudaStreamCaptureModeGlobal` (the committed code's choice) is documented CUDA behavior to make ALL process-wide stream activity, including other threads' concurrent use of the shared legacy stream 0, subject to that capture's exclusion rules -- with no barrier between phases, threads collided; the committed file checked zero CUDA return codes anywhere in the capture/launch sequence, so the resulting `cudaErrorStreamCaptureUnsupported`/`Implicit` failures were silent, reading exactly like a key-generation data race. (2) `cudaMalloc`/`cudaFree` (including inside `ks_context_create`/`ks_work_create`/`ks_context_destroy`/`ks_work_destroy`) are "potentially unsafe" CUDA APIs whose capture restriction is NOT fully scoped away by switching to `cudaStreamCaptureModeThreadLocal` alone -- unordered teardown let one thread's free invalidate another's in-flight capture (`cudaErrorStreamCaptureInvalidated`), again silently. This independently confirms, but relocates, Phase 30's own "legacy default stream 0... plausible but unconfirmed" hypothesis: real, but inside the TEST's scheduling of stream-0 usage against other threads' captures, not inside `keygen_host`/`evalkeygen_host_sold`'s own internals. Explains why Phase 30's two cache mutex fixes (`g_rtab`/`upc`, `ek_dev_tab`) could not have touched this: those guard host-side `std::map` races, an unrelated mechanism to CUDA's cross-thread capture-exclusion rules. **Fix, confined entirely to the test harness** (zero production source files touched, confirmed by `git diff --stat`): two spin-barriers (no thread begins capture until ALL threads finish their own setup+direct-call phase; no thread frees/destroys until ALL threads finish capture+launch+readback), `cudaStreamCaptureModeThreadLocal` + a non-blocking capture stream (defense in depth, not a substitute for the barriers), and explicit `cudaError_t` checking on every call in the sequence so a future regression fails loudly instead of silently returning stale buffer content. No mutex was added to `keygen_host`, `evalkeygen_host`, `ks_context_create`, `ks_work_create`, or `keyswitch_resident` -- there was no shared host state there to protect; per the governing directive's explicit "do not add a mutex without proof" instruction. Promoted the now-deterministic file to a real `ctest`-registered regression test. Full evidence trail, ownership map, and the resulting supported/unsupported concurrency contract: `research/SETUP_PATH_CONCURRENCY_INVESTIGATION.md`. | Fixed reproducer: 25/25 plain runs clean, 3/3 `compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck` runs clean (0 errors). Full `ctest` suite (excluding the memory-gated bootstrap tests, same convention since Phase 28): **113/123 passing** (up from 112/122 -- one newly-registered, newly-passing test), the exact same 10 pre-existing individually-documented failures as every prior phase, zero new regressions. No performance or memory impact measured or expected -- the change is confined to a non-production-path test file's own internal scheduling; `test_ks_graph_lifecycle`'s leak check (<=2 MiB VRAM drift/5 cycles) and `bench_ks_graph_perf`'s prior 1.49x warm-replay figure are both unaffected and the latter was not re-measured since nothing it depends on changed. | No production source file was modified: `src/keygen.cpp`, `src/evalkeygen.cpp`, `src/keyswitch_resident.cpp`, and every header under `include/` were read extensively (full stream-default-parameter and cache-mutex tracing) but zero lines of any were edited.
